Then Put in 800 in lot size step

If you treat both strategies to be one, compared to other stratgies, means that you run of of them at 50% risk while other stratgies are 100% risk. Therefore, you do not get more risk using them but benefit from diversification. 
20.05.2025 15:26 | Andrew Soma | even darwinex allows 0.95 correlation if I'm not mistaken

20.05.2025 15:41 | Till | You still understand me wrong. Let's assume we have 3 stratgies, a, b and c. b and c are highly correlated. Instead of trading them equally:a: 1 lotb: 1 lotc: 1 lotThen you can do this to reduce your risk while increasing diversification:a: 1 lotb: 0.5 lotc: 0.5 lotSo you treat b and c to be one strategy by adjusting their risk to be one compared to the other stratgy.

06.06.2025 15:13 | Ba Sa | @strueli how do we balance out a portfolio with multiple strategies, some using variableValue and some with fixed lotsize 
06.06.2025 15:21 |  | - Strats with fixedlotsize have much smaller DD amount due to...fixed lot size- Strats with variableValue have higher DD amount - and it's different per StratSo I got a hard time to figure out how to balance them out 
06.06.2025 15:53 | wim schrynemakers | you first have to run all strategies with fixed lotsize. 0.01lots for normal ones, and 0.1lots (or higher if 0.1lots is the minimum lotsize) for the ones using variable values and have "adjust lotsize to variable values" enabled.Then you have to "balance them, which means adjust each strategy's lotsize, so that they all have around the same max DD (in $). then you create the portfolio of those balanced strategies in QA.  then you check what the portfolio's Max DD was.  and based on that value and you max DD (for example "10% max DD"), you define the balance needed for that current portfolio.  so if the max DD for the portfolio is for example "1000$", and you want 10% max DD, that means your balance should be "10000$".  then you divide that 10000 for each strategy by it's lotsize factor (the factor being to reduce the lotsize  to 0.01lots).  so if the strategy has for example the balanced lotsize of 0.05lots in that portfolio, the lotsizeStep would be 10000/5 = 2000.  and you do that for each strategy.  And so you have the individual lotsizeStep for each strategy 
06.06.2025 16:14 | Ba Sa | Thanks. Let me try that. The latter part I understand. Just a little confused with "balance" the strategy with "variable values" enable, since lot size will be different in that particular strategy 
06.06.2025 16:19 | wim schrynemakers | the actual lotsize doesn't matter.  It matters what lotsize  you had set in the parameters 
06.06.2025 16:20 | Ba Sa | ahh. That makes sense. I understand it now. Thank you man 
06.06.2025 16:54 | Fred | isn’t a simpler way of balancing the portfolio to just use “risk per trade” and use the same % or $ risk for each trade? 
06.06.2025 16:59 |  | though i guess that doesn’t factor in that some strategies will have more or less trades and higher or lower win rates than other strategies. but if your strategies have similar number of trades and win rates, then that is probably the easiest way. 
06.06.2025 17:21 | Ba Sa | I think kinda similar. But you will have to run multiple time for each strats to find risk value for 10-20-30%  DD. Doing Wim's way we only run test once for a portfolio and easily find lotsize for each strats to accommodate 10-20-30-50% max DD 
06.06.2025 18:12 | wim schrynemakers | no, because having a lower risk per trade doesn't mean that the historical max DD will be lower as well.  it can be that a lower risk per trade is 2X more aggressive than another strategy with a higher risk per trade, so if you would make that "lower risk per trade" strategy more aggressive, you are even exponentially putting too much weight on that one.For me, the only metric that counts for balancing a portfolio, is the historical max DD per strategy. 
06.06.2025 18:25 | Fred | okay, that makes sense 
06.06.2025 18:30 |  | hey @strueli can i use the gold optimisation set file for the 1m and 5m?

06.06.2025 18:45 | wim schrynemakers | in theory you can,  but I just don't think scalping strategies are as stable as swing, and smaller timeframes have much more "noise", which will mean fake breakouts etc..  making a seperate optimization set won't do much help.  basically you'll need to decrease SL, TP and trailingSL values etc.. 
06.06.2025 18:51 | Fred | yes, but i do believe it could end up being profitable with the right settings, and with the volatility of gold it could present a lot of trades and therefore rapid growth. could you please customise a set file with those lower values so i can do the optimisation? i will freely share my resulting profitable sets with the group 
06.06.2025 19:25 | wim schrynemakers | Don't get me wrong, but the whole idea of the Ultimate breakout System, is that you also learn how to use it 😊  not that I simply make set files and optimization sets for every client.  it's very easy: you make the start/step/stop value (which are in pips), smaller than the ones in my original gold optimization sets.  so instead of starting SL for example from "5000", with step "1000" and stop "50000", you could for example start with "500", step 100 and stop "10000".  there are no "perfect" settings, and it might need a bit of trial an error, which I would need to do as well.  Also keep in mind, that for strategies for scalping, the difference between 1M OHLC and Real ticks will become bigger, so it might be that the optimization process gives you perfect strategies, when doing it with 1M OHLC, but that none of them will be worth anything when doing a real ticks test

14.07.2025 16:56 | wim schrynemakers | hi,I wouldn't recommend simply running each set at the same lotsize.  Because you will put much more weight on the strategies that trade a lot compared to the more conservative sets that don't trade much.The video I made regarding 'creating a balanced portfolio' explains exactly how to correctly set lotsizeStep for each of the strategies, or you can use it to calculate lotsize as well based on your balance.I did an example for my bitcoin sets some time ago, where I calculated the lotsizeStep for each strategy, in order to have max 10% total DD of that portfolio:bitcoin LotsizeStep 10% risk:d: 1490f: 151g: 319h: 7779i: 3585j: 3412K: 4073l: 4138m: 1355these are the lotsizeStep values for each one (this is for a USD account.  you must adjust for other currencies).if you want max 5% total DD, you should double each of those values (so each one will run at half the risk of my 10% max DD values)also, as you can see, strategy h has lotsizeStep 7779 already, which means that it needs AT LEAST 7779$ at 0.01lots to remain below 10%.  so to remain below 5%, you would need double that account size.(the biggest lotsizeStep value in the calculation can be used at minimum balance value)

24.07.2025 08:56 | wim schrynemakers | still don't understand your reasoning... lotsize is based on the different lotsizestep values, so the higher lotsize strategy, have a lower risk for the same lotsize, but  the "total risk" per strategy is about the same.  so we can directly use the "max drawdown" so far, to identify which strategies need to be evaluated.  And for evaluating, we can simply use fixed lotsize.  using lotsizeStep in the backtest only shifts "comparing $ drawdown" to "comparing % drawdown", nothing more.  and a backtest with LotsizeStep will have compounded growth, exponentially, which can cause things like "hitting max lotsize" in  the backtest, or even margin problem, which will make the evaluation incorrect.

17.07.2026 16:19 | Till | I started using ClaudeCode to automate my backtests recently, before that date I just used my python backtest and feature extraction scripts manually. Now I have automated all this, I just have to put set files in a inbox folder and give the AI some information about symbol and timeframe and it starts to backtests them using my tools, compares them to my existing sets and ranks them. It's quite a nice system and the best it that it kind of improves itself step-by-step. 
17.07.2026 16:21 | xInfinitYz | That's the way, I'm building an mcp for portfolio manager which will allow you to use the parallel backtesting capabilities with your flow 😁 
17.07.2026 16:21 | Till | Another benefit is that it produces quite nice optimization files for MT5 optimizer based on what it found to be working in other sets. 
17.07.2026 16:22 |  | Sounds great, especially as I'm one of your customer and have portfolio manager already :) 
17.07.2026 16:24 |  | The funny thing of using AI is that you give it the info US30-H1, it finds USTEC-M15 in the file, but it made a quick test on the fly to find out that the sets don't work with USTEC, so it adjusted them automatically before it put them into the backtest chain. 
17.07.2026 17:08 | UKrit | Wait , do you also give claudecode the historical data ? The one we can find on mt5 , history folder ? Is it claudecode better than Claudecowork? 
17.07.2026 17:12 | Till | Claude code does not use the historical data directly. It uses my python backtest scripts to start MT5 to run backtests automatically for certain sets, time ranges and modeling. I have not used cowork yet, so I can't say if its better or worse for this task. Claude code writes a lot of python scripts and updates it for its work, so I guess claude code is a good match here.

06.08.2026 22:10 |  | Working with AI is quite interesting, in my opinion. E.g. I pick only about 15 stratgies from one optimization following a certain scheme. I first asked the AI how it would pick them. Then I explained the AI how I did it in the past. The AI then did both methods, tested them and compared the results and we ended up with a hybrid where parts of my strategy are being used and part of the strategy the AI suggested,, which I had not in mind before. So its a real win, you basically have a companion to discuss things, and someone who does the tedious work. And it's great to tell it that its evening and he shall suggest what he wants to backtest on my PC during the night 😆 and in the morning you have a ready made table with results and a report for the nightly work.

22.08.2026 08:25 |  | Two new XAUUSD H1 sets: C1 and C6Built from my 323-set verification, not by optimizing: I took the 30 sets that passed the real-tick stage, analyzed how their entry/exit parameters are distributed, and designed two sets where the winners cluster. C1 is the center of that distribution (balanced). C6 is its aggressive edge: closer orders, tighter stop, twice the trades, higher drawdown. Both checked in-sample 2013-2023, out-of-sample 2024-2026 and on real ticks. GA and response-surface attempts didn't beat the plain winner median, so I stopped there.As shipped (0.1 lots + Adjust to Variable Values, $100k, TitanFX, no losing year 2013-2026):C1: 1M 2013-2026: NP $30.5k / PF 1.70 / RF 19.7. Real ticks last 12 months: NP $3.4k / PF 2.56 / RF 10.6.C6: 1M 2013-2026: NP $43.2k / PF 1.47 / RF 24.3. Real ticks last 12 months: NP $4.8k / PF 2.12 / RF 7.7.HistoricalMaxDD is filled in (C1 116.4 / C6 133.4 per 0.01 lots), unique magic numbers. Caveat: the winner ranges came from the same 2025-2026 tick window, so the tick numbers aren't fully out-of-sample. Forward testing ongoing. [FILE: C6_tick_as-shipped_0.1lot_2025-08_2026-08.png]

24.07.2025 09:18 | weilun | Yea but i think u kinda misunderstood me still tho, i understand using fixedlots as an analysis tool, but mu original point was it kindalook "downplayed" on dd, but i understand where ure coming from 
24.07.2025 09:30 | wim schrynemakers | Another thing to think about: since most of us, including me, are running many of the bitcoin strategies at 0.01lots, the risk per trade is actually higher now than in the past, because BTC price is a lot higher.  so the max DD in the past would be bigger with current SL values.  I hope this make sense to you?  so to get an accurate view on "how is current drawdown compared to historical one", we must make sure our backtest is not run at 0.01lots.  and it also means that drawdown might exceed the historical one (in terms of %) if you run at 0.01lots and btc price keeps going up.  drawdown for the same losses would now be 2X the drawdown for those same losses when BTC was around 60000 if we  run at 0.01lots.  this is because of the "variable values", which makes SL bigger in size when BTC rises in price 
24.07.2025 09:32 |  | (the backtests I just send were done with 0.1lots by the way, which were also  "variable" during the backtest to keep the same risk.  So in the end of the backtest, here were reduced automatically to 0.08, and in the beginning of the backtest, they were 0.34) 
24.07.2025 09:32 |  | all sounds very confusing maybe, but it's actually very good money management 
24.07.2025 09:32 | weilun | Yea that "variable" factor clarifies it for me 
24.07.2025 09:34 |  | My point was  to apply comsistemt risk management, i thought u ran it at a fixedlot earlier 
24.07.2025 09:35 |  | Thanks btw :) 
24.07.2025 09:38 |  | Honestly, ur risk management methods are very well thought out, idk if many people actually really understands and appreciates that 
24.07.2025 10:19 | wim schrynemakers | thx! 
24.07.2025 10:53 | wim schrynemakers | ok, I think this is what you wanted to see: all strategies together, using Max 20% DD parameter, but fixed balance, so each strategy runs at its own lotsize.  So now we can compare current drawdown (without today's losses) to historical one of the full portfolio.  We are still very much in line with the backtest [FILE: image_2025-07-24_10-53-57.png]
24.07.2025 10:55 |  | keep in mind that I used a bigger balance (100K) so that the lotsize can really be run as it should.  on smaller balance, we are limited to 0.01lots as minimum lotsize, so some strategies might run at higher risk than we actually wanted.  not in this backtest

29.04.2026 08:09 | xInfinitYz | You bind an EA to a symbol so it can work, but if you use multiple strategies in onechart setup, the EURUSD is used as a placeholder to attach the chart to “something”, but under the hood the EA uses all the configured symbols from the .set files, usually EURUSD is recommended because it is the main chart for trading which has most liquidity etc 
29.04.2026 08:11 | wim schrynemakers | You need to put the symbol name in the set files that you are going to use in the OneChartSetup. For Eurusd strategies you put “EURUSD” or “EURUSD.ecn” if that is what your broker uses as symbol. For gold sets you fill in XAUUSD etc.  You don’t need to fill it in in the actual ea running the chart. It will load all the set files. This is ONLY when using the autoloader

26.08.2026 21:21 | M L | 5 looks pointless. The plateau is at 4 (at least a full 2026 real tick). The biggest jump is from 1 to 2 in pending orders, not in max trades (1% risk per trade; it is 23k to nearly 31k, 22 more trades). From there, 2 max trades instead of 1 gives an additional 2 trades. The next noticeable step is 4 max pending orders and 2 max trades, which gives a 31.8k profit with 248 trades (from the initial 220 to 248).So it seems that setting max pending orders from 1 to 2 yields the largest jump, while the rest is insignificant. According to backtests run through optimization.

02.09.2026 18:29 |  | Two sets from my live portfolio - and for once, no gold: JP225 H1 and USDJPY H1.How they were made, in one line: I don't hand-optimize. I sweep a few hundred random parameter combos over 10 years of 1-minute data, keep only the regions where most combos are profitable (not just one lucky pass), then make the survivors pass several real-tick tests before anything is allowed into my own live account. These two passed.📈 UBS-JP225-H1-SPD-20260903-01S/R breakout, both directions, magic 991008• Real ticks 2025.08-2026.08: PF 2.40, 136 trades, 10/13 months green• 1-min 2018-2026: PF 1.39, 1,295 trades, only losing year 2019 (small)• HistoricalMaxDD 0.45 USD at 0.01 lots. 03.09.2026 03:17 | SpadaRosso | I am also one of those who use AI to optimize UBS portfolios, but it is possible to find promising parameter regions even without using MT5's GA. In the past I have done that optimization on a laptop PC as well, so a powerful CPU or GPU is not necessarily required. What matters most is not discovering a single mutation-like parameter, but discovering a statistically significant parameter plateau, and for that, the design of the optimization pipeline is important. As far as I have checked so far, apart from XAUUSD, the four instruments for which I have been able to discover and confirm the existence of a statistically significant parameter plateau are USTEC, JP225, HK50, and USDJPY.With brokers that have high trading costs, such as TitanFX, BTCUSD and XAGUSD cannot maintain a statistical edge. 
03.09.2026 03:26 |  | Since I'm selecting only what survives under TitanFX's trading costs, I expect the statistical edge to be even stronger on most of the brokers you're using. 
03.09.2026 03:34 |  | Since I'm Japanese, let me add some context specific to the yen. The Japanese government is wary of further yen weakening and has intervened in the FX market repeatedly. If you look at the USDJPY equity curve I shared, you'll notice pulse-like spikes — I believe those are driven by government intervention.Unfortunately, the yen's decline isn't stopping, because the rate differential with the dollar isn't narrowing. In other words, further intervention on USDJPY is likely, which makes it a good fit for UBS. The same applies to JP225, which is strongly correlated with the yen.That's why JP225 and USDJPY made it through my evaluation pipeline. I'd say these are symbols where the positives for UBS line up not only technically but fundamentally as well. 
03.09.2026 08:50 | Tim dV | Do us usew Python or something to gind that statistically significant parameter plateau? Or do you feed AI your optimization results? 
03.09.2026 09:14 | SpadaRosso | I'm doing the search in Python, though the code itself was generated by AI. The goal isn't to find a single optimal point, but to identify a robust region of the parameter space. If I had to give the optimization pipeline a mathematical name, it would be something like "level-set estimation of a response surface via space-filling experimental design (LHS)." 
03.09.2026 10:08 | SpadaRosso | Sharing a short write-up: "Hunting for Plateaus" (PDF, 5 pages).It's the optimization method behind my sets - why picking the best backtest is a trap, and why we hunt for regions where every nearby setting wins instead. Written for zero background, with real numbers, including two of our own results that looked near-certain and were pure luck.Curve-fitting is the one enemy we all share. Hope this helps someone's next set. [FILE: Hunting_for_Plateaus_202609.pdf]

03.09.2026 11:15 | BK | What i generally do is logically optimize 1 or 2 values and see which values are sharing the same result. If value 1 to 10 is optimized the strategy is profitable on value 3-7 then a good value would be 5. Then keep checking different values until complete. Also i try to avoid starting with trails and breakeven.

17.09.2026 21:49 | Zairex_ | Zairex Community Pack  14 UBS sets with re-tuned exitsWe all started from the same 320 set files, so I won't pretend these are new strategies. They're the same strategies with the exits re-tuned. Entries, filters and sizing are untouched  exactly as the original authors built them. Only the 10 exit settings changed.How they were tested, so you can judge them properly:Each strategy was tuned on 2021.01.01 – 2024.03.01, then both versions  the original and mine were backtested on 2024.03.08 – 2026.09.14, a period the tuning never saw. A set only made this pack if it came out ahead there. Everything was run on Vantage Markets at 100% real ticks.All of them loaded together on one $100,000 account, over that unseen period:- Net profit +53,296 (+53%)- Worst equity drawdown 4,871 (4.35%)- Profit factor 1.74, 3,984 trades, 69% wonI also backtested every set on its own over the same period. The 14 individual results add up to 53,207, against 53,296 for the one-account run  within a tenth of a percent. So nothing in here depends on the others being loaded.Compared with their own originals, across the 14: profit factor is higher in 13 of 14, drawdown is lower in 11 of 14, and profit per unit of drawdown is a median ×1.79 better.Full breakdown,The download includes the same page as an offline HTML file, plus all my raw MT5 reports so you can check the numbers yourself.Before you load them two things that will cost you money if you skip them:1. Set overrideAutoLoadLotsSettings = false in the AutoLoader. Leave it on and the AutoLoader overrides each set's lot settings, and you're no longer trading what was tested.2. Check the symbol names. These use XAUUSD+, BTCUSD and my broker's naming. If yours differs, fix ForceSymbol first or the AutoLoader silently trades nothing.Magic numbers 9639001–9639015 are reserved for this pack, and the trade comments start with Zairex, so you can spot them in the terminal.These are backtests, not promises. Run them on demo first and judge for yourself. Zairex
